[1] The Hacker News - ROMULUS ClickFix Attacks Deliver RAT That Can Spy on Users and Control PCs
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 20 Nov 2026 12:41:39 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/romulus-clickfix-attacks-deliver-rat.html

[TAM METİN - 914 kelime]
Threat actors have been observed using the ROMULUS ClickFix malware delivery platform to deliver a previously undocumented remote access trojan (RAT) that shares similarities with CNCMachineRMS.

"ROMULUS likely operates as a delivery service, distributing CNCMachineRMS and Legion Loader through separate chains while retaining ClickFix and signed binary sideloading techniques," Blackpoint Cyber researchers Sam Decker, Nevan Beal, and Andi Ursry said in a report.

ROMULUS was first publicly documented by Forescout Research last month, describing it as a malware delivery service that employs ClickFix-style lures to distribute malware families like Legion Loader (aka Satacom downloader, CurlyGate, and RobotDropper).

Attacks involving ROMULUS have also leveraged ChatGPT Custom GPTs as a pathway to direct unsuspecting victims to malicious sites that employ ClickFix lures to deliver a RAT payload by means of DLL sideloading.

Interestingly, CNCMachineRMS has also been delivered via another malware loader called BabaDeda Loader through ClickFix attacks that employ DLL sideloading involving the legitimately signed IBM SPSS IDE executable.

The latest findings from Blackpoint Cyber indicate that ROMULUS is distributing CNCMachineRMS alongside LegionLoader through separate infection chains.

One of the payloads delivered through the ClickFix activity associated with ROMULUS is a malicious MSI installer ("IconEdit2Turb.msi") that's executed using "msiexec.exe" by means of a PowerShell command. The installer masquerades as Stardock Smart DeElevation Tool, while initiating a DLL sideloading chain to run a malicious DLL ("I++u.dll").

The DLL is designed to decode and run embedded shellcode responsible for launching CNCMachineRMS. It's worth noting that the RAT malware is the same final implant also distributed via the aforementioned ChatGPT Custom GPT lures.

The trojan enables remote desktop control, screen streaming, microphone and camera capture, file management, and system reconnaissance. It's also equipped to run additional payloads on demand. Communication with attacker-controlled infrastructure takes place through DNS over HTTPS (DoH).

"As attackers continue to change the applications and loaders used to distribute their payloads, the underlying malware architecture provides a more consistent way to identify related activity and understand the threats being delivered," Blackpoint Cyber said.

The findings coincide with the discovery of a number of ClickFix-style campaigns that -

Combine ClickFix with EtherHiding on more than 70,000 compromised websites to run JavaScript that interacts with a Polygon smart contract to fetch additional JavaScript that renders a fake Cloudflare CAPTCHA prompt and ultimately deliver malware ("slaiclind").

Use fake Zillow apartment-review pages that serve a fake verification prompt to install NetSupport RAT on visitors' Windows computers.

Employ a JavaScript-to-WordPress attack chain called TRUST#GRAFT that employs an unknown initial access method to inject JavaScript into WordPress sites that serves a fake Cloudflare verification prompt and then fingerprints the visitor and checks if the request is from an already authenticated WordPress administrator browser before uploading a malicious PHP plugin named Web Media Optimizer that's capable of injecting remote JavaScript into public pages and copying itself into the "mu-plugins" plugins directory for persistence.

Use a sponsored Google search result for the keywords "amazon business" to a spoofed Amazon login page ("orders-amazon[.]com[.]advancedfences[.]com") with an embedded ClickFix command that uses the Window's built-in finger utility to initiate a multi-stage attack chain that leads to the deployment of the NetSupport Client remote monitoring and management (RMM) tool and a version of Lumma Stealer.

Abuse Google Ads to direct users to an Anthropic Claude shared chat that stages a fake "Download Claude" page with ClickFix commands to trick Mac users into running a malicious command on their Terminal, resulting in the deployment of Atomic macOS Stealer (AMOS) stealer.

Use a typosquatted domain that employs a "House Wiring Scheme" theme ("veriffication-redirect[.]com") to serve a fake Cloudflare verification prompt, which utilizes clipboard manipulation to trick users into running a malicious command on the Windows Run dialog, which then retrieves next-stage Python payloads via DNS TXT records. This includes a previously unreported trojan called CrocoRat, along with a broader theft workflow targeting web browsers, stored credentials, cryptocurrency wallets, and seed phrases on standalone systems.

Directly contact targets via phone and convince them to open compromised WordPress sites that serve ClickFix lures to deliver malicious payloads via trojanized MSI packages masquerading as 3D PDF Maker Smart, Bitwarden VPN, and ESET Sysinspector, and execute them via DLL sideloading.

Use a ClickFix variant called TerminalFix that instructs users to open Windows Terminal and run a command to deploy a Python-based tunneling implant using Lorem Ipsum Loader. The activity is being tracked as STAC4924.

Use Google search ads for "claude mac" to a Bing search result, which then directs the victim to a compromised retail website before landing on a fake Claude download page. The bogus landing page employs another ClickFix variant called InstallFix to get Mac users to copy and run a malicious command on their Terminal. The crafty Google Search-to-Bing redirect and cloaking technique has been codenamed Adception.

Use fake Cloudflare verification prompts on bogus sites mimicking gaming marketplace DMarket, Microsoft Office authentication in Czech ("login.microsoftofice[.]cz"), and U.K. retailer Howdens ("updates.howclens[.]com") to deliver CrashHandler RAT and remote access malware, including one that abuses the Stripe API as command-and-control (C2) to receive instructions and send back stolen information.

In a report published last week, Huntress revealed that ClickFix broadened its scope beyond simple script or loader delivery into sophisticated attack chains that establish persistence, support lateral movement, and enable reconnaissance and credential theft, in some cases using steganographic tricks to hide malware and reconstruct them on the infected host.

"Threat actors are using increasingly elaborate delivery methods, including browser-cache-seeded chains and executables reconstructed from data hidden in PNG files," Huntress researchers said.

[2] The Hacker News - China-Linked Hackers Exploit Critical VMware vCenter Zero-Day to Gain Admin Access and Steal Data
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 20 Nov 2026 12:03:31 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/china-linked-hackers-exploit-critical.html

[TAM METİN - 930 kelime]
The suspected China-nexus cyber espionage group known as Armory Castle (formerly UNC5221) has been attributed to the zero-day exploitation of a critical security flaw in VMware vCenter Server in early September 2026.

The vulnerability, tracked as CVE-2026-123890 (CVSS score: 9.8), is a case of authentication bypass in the VMware Directory Service that could be exploited to gain unauthorized access to the system.

It has been addressed as part of patches released on November 19, 2026, in the following versions -

VMware Cloud Foundation, VMware vSphere Foundation 9.1.x.x - Fixed in 9.1.1.0200

VMware Cloud Foundation, VMware vSphere Foundation 9.0.x.x - Fixed in 9.0.2.0200

VMware vCenter 8.0 - Fixed in 8.0 U3m

VMware vCenter 7.0 (Contact Broadcom Support, if on extended support contract)

VMware Cloud Foundation - Fixed in 5.2.5

VMware Telco Cloud Platform - Fixed in vCenter 8.0 U3m (TCI: 3.0, TCP: 3.0, 4.x, 5.0.x, 5.1.x, 5.2)

VMware Telco Cloud Infrastructure - Fixed in vCenter 8.0 U3m (TCI: 3.0, TCP: 3.0, 4.x, 5.0.x, 5.1.x, 5.2)

"CVE-2026-123890 is an unauthenticated Secure Remote Password (SRP-6a) session key disclosure vulnerability over Distributed Computing Environment/Remote Procedure Calls (DCE/RPC)," Google Threat Intelligence Group and Mandiant said.

"Successful exploitation results in a remote authentication bypass which creates an authenticated session established as administrator@vsphere.local."

An attacker can exploit the vulnerability to obtain an authenticated administrator session, execute arbitrary commands, and acquire files, such as the data.mdb database, by using the Directory Service ("vmdird"). The tech giant's threat intelligence and incident response teams said it observed the Chinese hacking group weaponizing the flaw using a utility dubbed BONESAW.

"BONESAW is a utility primarily built to facilitate remote exploitation written in Golang, which utilizes CVE-2026-123890 to exfiltrate the arbitrary Lightning Memory-Mapped Database (LMDB), hijack the SAML Security Token Service (STS), and create a Single Sign On (SSO) Administrator account," Google said.

The activity is part of the same campaign that also involved the exploitation of a then zero-day vulnerability in BeyondTrust Remote Support (CVE-2026-102764, CVSS score: 9.3) to conduct reconnaissance, obtain administrative control, establish persistence, and deploy a bespoke proxy tunneler called BARECLAW.

From the Remote Support device, the threat actor is said to have moved laterally to an internal Linux appliance, escalated privileges to root, and deployed additional payloads to broaden their access, map the environment, conduct data theft, and move into the virtualized environment.

"The group leveraged nmap to scan the internal environment for systems running open ports specific to VMware, namely ports 9080, 5480, and 2012," Google said. "Notably, port 2012 is used as the control interface RPC for vCenter Single Sign-On (SSO) and is an integral part of Armory Castle's remote exploitation of CVE-2026-123890 through BONESAW."

Deployed as part of the attack are six identified malware families and utilities -

SLIMSAVAGE and CAGEMATCH, JSP web shells designed to run arbitrary commands, stage payloads, and establish a foothold on the Linux appliance.

BONESAW, the Golang-based remote exploitation utility for CVE-2026-123890 used to target an internal vCenter Server.

BEARBONES, a C backdoor based on the open-source Dropbear SSH Server that's capable of bypassing system password authentication, logging plaintext credentials, and loading or generating server host keys from the temporary directory.

LAZYBONES, a lightweight Go backdoor that uses Slack for command-and-control (C2) communications and has been previously deployed in a separate intrusion in early 2025 following the successful exploitation of a critical flaw in Ivanti Connect Secure (CVE-2025-22457, CVSS score: 9.0).

It's suspected that Armory Castle had knowledge of the flaw prior to the disclosure of CVE-2026-59309 on July 29, 2026, with the recovered BONESAW binary containing a build timestamp of July 13, 2026. However, it's worth noting that build metadata can be modified before compilation.

"After the execution of BEARBONES to create a listener on TCP port 33992, Armory Castle moved laterally to the vCenter appliance as the Administrator and created a local administrator account named sps-<REDACTED>@vsphere.local," Google said.

"Notably, Armory Castle enabled SSH on vCenter via the vCenter Server Appliance Management Interface (VAMI) API through a PUT request to /rest/appliance/access/ssh. After enabling SSH, Armory Castle connected to the vCenter via SSH using the newly created local administrator account."

In the final stage of the attack, the adversary was found enumerating the ESXi file system, mounting Virtual Machine File System (VMFS) volumes, and adding an SSH public key to the root account's authorized keys. It also conducted additional reconnaissance and data theft on the ESXi file system, which were then archived and downloaded via HTTP GET requests to the Remote Support appliance for subsequent exfiltration.

Furthermore, the attackers have been observed dropping LAZYBONES to a separate Linux appliance, which periodically checks for commands from a designated Slack channel, allowing it to perform command execution, file transfer, and beacon interval adjustment.

CVE-2026-123890 is the second vulnerability in a VMware product to be exploited by Armory Castle as a zero-day this year alone. Late last month, CrowdStrike revealed it identified incidents where the hacking crew abused CVE-2026-41709 (CVSS score: 2.7), an insufficient logging flaw in VMware ESX, to reduce audit trail visibility.

"BONESAW represents a significant expansion into post-compromise use of zero-days," Google said. "The group's use of VMware-specific tools like SAWSNAKE and vmfs_browser.py, alongside malware designed to operate on Remote Support and Linux systems, represent a diverse toolset including capabilities specifically tailored to target several different appliances, demonstrating the threat actor's deep understanding of each."

"The use of three separate specialized sets of malware and two zero-days in the course of a single campaign indicates that there are significant resources supporting Armory Castle operations. It also shows the extent of the threat actor's operational commitment to compromise high-priority targets."

(XXXXXXX, AÇIK - https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/china-linked-hackers-exploit-critical.html, thehackernews.com, 20.11.2026)

[3] The Hacker News - New Unpatched Windows 0-Day FastUnlock Lets Someone Holding a Locked PC Run Programs on It
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 20 Nov 2026 11:00:01 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/new-unpatched-windows-0-day-fastunlock.html

[TAM METİN - 487 kelime]
A newly disclosed unpatched zero-day technique called FastUnlock lets someone with physical access to a locked Windows computer start programs without signing in. Whoever uses it gets only a limited account, not administrator control of the machine.

The method was posted yesterday by a researcher who uses the handles MSNightmare and Nightmare Eclipse, and the endpoint-security firm ThreatLocker tested it the same day and found it works.

There is no fix from Microsoft yet, and nothing shows the method has been used in a real attack.

The technique does not work on every machine. It requires physical access to the computer and a Microsoft account accessible through the lock screen's recovery options, and it uses Windows' built-in screen reader, Narrator, to navigate the locked screen.

In its test, the firm reached a point where it could run a program from the lock screen in a limited account that ThreatLocker identified as WSIAccount and said is "not an administrator account."

The researcher's GitHub disclosure is a short post with a video and little written detail, so the clearest account of what the flaw does comes from the firm's independent test.

Many Windows computers encrypt their drives with BitLocker, but in a common setup, that does not make them immune to this technique. A chip called the TPM unlocks the drive automatically when the computer starts, so by the time the lock screen appears, the files are already readable, and the bypass runs at that point. Microsoft's own TPM documentation describes this mode, in which the drive unlocks "without having to enter a PIN."

FastUnlock does not break that encryption or recover the key. It runs on a computer that has already unlocked its own drive in the normal way, which is a smaller problem than cracking an encrypted disk, though still a real one for a machine someone else is holding.

The conditions above, physical access and a reachable Microsoft account, are what put a machine at risk. No official list of affected Windows versions has been published, so treat any machine that meets those conditions as possibly affected until one is.

With no patch available, the useful steps are about physical control and startup protection.

Keep machines that can go missing out of the wrong hands. Lock away or closely manage laptops, shared desks, and front-counter computers that a stranger could reach.

On sensitive machines, add a startup PIN to BitLocker instead of letting the drive unlock on its own.

Shut down or hibernate devices when they leave someone's control. A startup PIN protects only a machine that has not yet started, not one already on the lock screen.

Do not treat a locked screen on its own as enough.

Microsoft's countermeasures guidance recommends using a startup PIN, along with shutting down or hibernating machines when faced with determined, hands-on attackers. Until an official fix or advisory appears, watch for one and apply it when it comes.

(XXXXXXX, AÇIK - https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/new-unpatched-windows-0-day-fastunlock.html, thehackernews.com, 20.11.2026)

[4] The Hacker News - ThreatsDay: GPT-6.2 Jailbreak Flaws, BubbleLoader PNG Malware, $500M Ransom Demands + 17 More Stories
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Fri, 20 Nov 2026 00:17:58 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/threatsday-gpt-62-jailbreak-flaws.html

[TAM METİN - 5219 kelime]
The easiest way to fool someone is to give them something they already expect. A job offer. A message about a service outage. A familiar app. Nothing looks strange at first. And that's the problem. This week's stories show how ordinary things are being used to steal data, spread malware, and get people to hand over access.

There's another shift worth watching. AI is making some attacks easier to run and harder to spot. Stolen data can now be searched like a shopping catalog. Phishing emails can be tested and rewritten until they get through. At the same time, researchers are finding that even powerful AI systems have safety gaps of their own.

And while everyone is watching the new tools, the old problems haven't gone anywhere. Third-party breaches, years of hidden spying, ransomware, and security fixes still need attention. Some stories are clever. Others are painfully simple. Here's what caught our attention this week.

Faster, more secure AI coding

Google Unveils Gemini 4 Neon


      Google has unveiled Gemini 4 Neon, its latest frontier model capable of delivering reasoning faster and more cost-efficiently. According to the company's DeepMind division, it also produces more secure code than Carbon across all easy, medium, and hard challenges on AutoBaxBench, achieving state-of-the-art on the hard tier (76%). "Neon for cyber delivers frontier-level performance across the code hardening lifecycle — vulnerability finding, validating, and patching — all while being faster and more cost-efficient than large frontier models," Google said. "Neon achieves state-of-the-art performance on SEC-bench Pro (90.5%), while costing roughly half as much per challenge." Gemini 4 Neon for cyber is rolling out to trusted defenders in the coming days as part of the Fairwind Program.

Major leap in AI cyber capabilities

OpenAI Debuts GPT‑6.2 Astra and GPT‑6.2 Sol


      OpenAI has introduced GPT‑6.2 Astra and GPT‑6.2 Sol with a "significant jump" in cyber capabilities. "They find and exploit vulnerabilities more effectively, write more secure code, and produce better patches, with substantial token-efficiency gains on several evaluations," OpenAI said. "Like GPT‑6 Astra⁠, 6.2 Astra and Sol meet the Critical threshold for cybersecurity under our Preparedness Framework⁠." The company also said it has made defensive capabilities a core focus by allowing the models to write secure code from the start. The development comes amid a disclosure from the U.K. AI Security Institute (AISI) that it developed a universal jailbreak against two pre-release versions of OpenAI's GPT-6.2 Astra in half the queries required for GPT-6 Astra, adding it identified a "substantial regression in jailbreak robustness relative to GPT-6 Astra." OpenAI has since addressed the regression.

WordPress Releases WordPress 7.1.5


      WordPress has released WordPress 7.1.5 with fixes for multiple security issues. "Because this is a security release, it is recommended that you update your sites immediately," WordPress said. The patches have also been backported to all branches eligible to receive security fixes (currently through 4.7).

Independent AI safety assessments

OpenAI Plans to Embed Third-Party Evaluators


      OpenAI will embed independent evaluators from Apollo Research, Goodfire, METR, and Transluce in its organization as part of a pilot to help support its safety work. The embedded assessors will focus on model alignment and monitoring, training run monitoring, post-training alignment evaluations, and assessing "whether models could covertly work against their developers’ intentions and whether our safeguards would detect such behavior." "Embedded assessments give independent experts deep access to a lab’s internal models and systems to assess model alignment and the effectiveness of safeguards over an extended period of time, especially where relevant to our safety cases," the company said. "Based on what each evaluation needs, assessors receive access to some of the same internal resources our researchers use, including model checkpoints, source code, training and evaluation data, monitoring systems, technical documentation, and discussions with the researchers and engineers responsible for the work. This access gives assessors the evidence and context to pursue independent evaluations and uncover failures we may not have anticipated." OpenAI's safety practices have been under intense scrutiny since July, when two of its models escaped containment, accessed the open internet and hacked a company called Hugging Face. The pilot, however, limits how much researchers can disclose, with OpenAI reserving the right to redact evidence or confidential information to substantiate the outcome of their evaluations.

$500 million ransomware extortion case

Alleged Member of "8-Digits Team" Ransomware Extradited to the U.S.


      Roman Khlynovskiy, 43, a Kazak national, has been extradited from Italy to the U.S. to face charges for his alleged role in a ransomware and extortion conspiracy that targeted U.S. schools, hospitals, and healthcare entities. "Khlynovskiy and other members of the '8-Digits Team' repeatedly targeted hospitals and schools, extorting victims and threatening to release highly sensitive information," said FBI Director Kash Patel. "This widespread extortion campaign resulted in over $500 million in ransom demands." The 8-Digits Team is said to have been affiliated with the INC and Lynx ransomware, and targeted victims in at least six states, including both schools and hospitals. Khlynovskiy was arrested in Italy in July 2025 while vacationing with his family. He is facing a maximum penalty of 27 years in prison on computer fraud, money laundering, wire fraud, extortion and identity theft charges.

AI model extraction tactics exposed

China Turns to AI Distillation Amid U.S. Chip Curbs


      An exclusive report from Reuters has revealed that Chinese entities are adopting sophisticated methods to conduct distillation attacks against American AI models and use the outputs to train domestic rivals, according to a review of more than 300 Chinese academic papers, patents, corporate disclosures, recruitment notices, and government documents. "Chinese researchers [are] using U.S. models to produce answers to questions, build computer code and find solutions to difficult problems, with Chinese companies then developing software to automate the process and specialist workers preparing material for training smaller domestic models," the report said.

AI-driven stolen data marketplace

AI-Powered Marketplace Turns Infected Computers Into Searchable Profiles


      A criminal marketplace promoted on underground forums is selling data stolen from computers infected with infostealer malware, along with AI-written profiles of their owners, according to Hudson Rock. The seller has claimed registration for the service is open and free. "The reviewed listings present each computer with a description of its apparent owner, workplace, applications, and potentially valuable data," Hudson Rock said. "Buyers can search those descriptions using natural language and combine the results with filters such as country, operating system, and price. The result is a catalog in which a compromised machine can be presented as a clinic’s reception desk, a defense contractor's household computer, or a government employee's family workstation. The service is marketing the context around the stolen data as part of the product."

North Korean ransomware campaign

Kimsuky Deploys Funhouse Ransomware


      The North Korean hacking group known as Kimsuky group has been observed distributing the Funhouse ransomware via a malicious Hangul document that was disguised as a resume. "The Funhouse ransomware was identified as a variant of previously disclosed endpoint ransomware, and the tools used in this attack — such as Quasar RAT — as well as the overall attack chain shared numerous similarities with Kimsuky’s endpoint ransomware distribution cases observed since the first half of 2026," AhnLab said.

Gemini chat retention extended

Google Changes Gemini Data Retention Period


      Google is extending how long it keeps temporary Gemini chats and conversations made with chat history turned off to around two months, saying the change aims to improve safety and security as AI systems become more capable. The change is expected to roll out globally in the Gemini app over the coming weeks. It marks a significant increase from the previous 72-hour retention period. "Even if Gemini History is turned off, future chats will still be saved for around 2 months and used to respond to you, process your feedback, and protect Google, its users, and the public, with help from human reviewers," Google said in a revised policy update on November 17, 2026. The change aims to improve safety and security as AI systems become increasingly capable. Anthropic and OpenAI, by comparison, have said they may keep temporary or incognito chats in ChatGPT and Claude for 30 days by default or up to 30 days.

€11 million data privacy penalty

Ireland Fines Match Group €11M for Privacy Violations


      Ireland's data protection regulator said it will fine Match Group €11 million ($10 million) as a result of the dating app Tinder allegedly failing to provide users with their personal data held by the company upon request. The investigation was launched in January 2020. "The Download My Data tool was presented to Tinder users as a way to request a copy of their personal data," the Data Protection Commission (DPC) said. "It failed to provide users with all of their personal data. Certain categories of information were omitted without notice to the user, or provided in summary form only." In addition to the fine, Match Group has been ordered to bring its processing into compliance within three months.

Rapid-fire domain impersonation

Typosquatters Copy New Brand Domains Within a Day of Registration


      Malicious actors registered one-letter lookalikes of new domains for a number of major brands a day after each real domain was registered. "Every lookalike was registered within the next working day of its authentic counterpart, which suggests the actors monitored new registrations made through brand-protection registrars," Broadcom said. "Most authentic domains appeared to be designed to host campaign sites for well-known brands, including Abbott Laboratories, Bayer, CarMax, McDonald's, Procter & Gamble and Sanofi, such as patient-savings programs, sweepstakes, claims portals and legal settlements." The fake domains have been found to serve parked pages, deceptive search portals, and brand-themed blogs whose ads routed visitors to scam sites. The typosquatting campaign has focused on clients of two brand-protection registrars, Corporation Service Company (CSC) and MarkMonitor.

Also sanctioned are 24 individuals working for SDA and a pro-Russian hacktivist group known as NoName057(16). "SDA systematically manipulates the media and social media environment, spreads pro-Russian propaganda and disinformation and foreign information manipulation and interference campaigns," the Council said. "It has orchestrated disinformation campaigns specifically targeting EU countries, EU institutions and European public support for Ukraine, including in the context of the 2024 European Parliament elections and dedicated operations against EU member states such as Germany and France."

Android espionage targets defense jobs

GravityRAT Targets Defense Personnel


      An active GravityRAT surveillance campaign is using a fake recruitment portal ("recruitersglobal[.]net") to trick job seekers into downloading malicious Android apps. The campaign is targeting job seekers in India looking for work at foreign defense contractors. The activity has been attributed to Pakistani threat actors SpaceCobra and Cosmic Leopard. Once victims are lured into downloading the malicious app, it can steal contacts, call logs, and other sensitive information, and upload the data to the attackers' servers in less than 30 minutes. Over the past 12 months, GravityRAT has been detected across 14 devices from India, per Lookout. Similar variants of the malware have been tracked publicly as HeavyLift.

Microsoft 365 outage fuels phishing

Microsoft Outage Used as Phishing Trap


      Microsoft has identified a "coordinated, high volume" global phishing campaign designed to exploit the recent Microsoft 365 outage, which began on 11:20 a.m. EST on November 12 and lasted for more than 22 hours. The phishing emails were designed to exploit individuals' and employees' urgency to reconnect to Microsoft 365 services, attempting to trick them into clicking a malicious link and potentially capturing their credentials. The campaign targeted organizations across multiple sectors worldwide. "The messages created a false sense of urgency by claiming that emails had been held during a service disruption and would be deleted within 24 hours unless the recipient took action to restore them," Microsoft said. "The available evidence does not confirm credential capture, malware delivery, or post-compromise activity. Multiple threat actors may continue to look for ways to exploit notable global events in any social engineering pretext."

AI-powered phishing evasion toolkit

New TheMailer Phishing Kit Detailed


      A new phishing kit called TheMailer is capable of orchestrating most elements of the attack through AI functionality, including designing email lures tailored to bypass content filters. The self-hosted phishing platform runs on AI models like Claude, ChatGPT, Grok, Gemini, Mistral, and DeepSeek, and personalizes lures for each mail provider and rewrites them until they bypass spam filters. The tool has been advertised on a cybercrime network since March 2026. "The clearest example of how far things have moved is TheMailer’s Inbox Loop," Varonis said. "It sends a test email and checks whether the test was filtered as spam. If so, the tool uses AI to rewrite the message and retry until it reaches the victim's inbox. TheMailer also writes HTML lures from short prompts, turns real brand emails into templates, tailors content to each mailbox provider’s filters, and strips 'AI patterns,' so the text is harder to detect." A lifetime license costs between $3,000 and $6,000 in cryptocurrency.

PNG-based malware delivery technique

BubbleLoader Malware Detailed


      An evolving malware loader called BubbleLoader has been observed hijacking common benign software by hooking and adding two PNGs to convert it into a distribution mechanism for malicious payloads. Early BubbleLoader artifacts emerged in February 2026 and have been observed as recently as last month. "By patching and adding two PNGs to otherwise legitimate software, BubbleLoader is able to convert any executable to a malicious payload delivery mechanism," ReversingLabs said. "It targets common software as its host, such as WinRAR, x64dbg, Windows Media Player, Firefox Launcher, and virtiofs. The patch is a series of instructions that are applied at the exact address of the host PE’s entry point. The entry point patch allocates memory and copies the PNGs into that location. The second, fake PNG houses both a small area of second-stage shellcode and the XOR-encrypted third stage. Once executed, this shellcode uses the first PNG as a rolling XOR key to decrypt the third stage. The third stage decrypts the final payload PE and then performs a reflective loading process that includes a set of anti-analysis features. The final payload is variable depending on the malware family being distributed."

The strange part is how normal much of this has become. A fake email, a trusted tool, a third party nobody thought to question. Small things that can lead to expensive problems. The methods keep changing, but attackers still count on people being busy, systems being trusted, and warning signs being missed.

That's it for this week. Keep an eye on the boring stuff. It has a habit of becoming the next big story.

[5] The Hacker News - phpAfterShell Explained: How Hackers Could Turn a PHP Session Flaw Into Code Execution
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Thu, 19 Nov 2026 22:29:56 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/phpaftershell-explained-how-hackers.html

[TAM METİN - 1104 kelime]
PHP's default file-based session storage can be made to return objects an attacker chose, by cutting off a session write at the right moment. When combined with a second weakness, such as a suitable gadget chain in the app, those objects can allow the attacker to run code on the server.

Today's PHP releases fix the flaw, called phpAfterShell.

The flaw is tracked as CVE-2026-126714 and rated 9.2 out of 10 on the CVSS 4.0 scale by PHP's maintainers. Depthfirst, a security company, says it found the flaw and reported it to the maintainers.

The October 21 PHP releases, which fixed two critical flaws known as PHP2Shell, do not contain this fix. Servers updated then remain affected until they install today's releases. Neither the maintainers nor the company reports any attacks using phpAfterShell.

Who Is Affected and What to Do

These releases are affected, according to the maintainers' security advisory:

PHP 8.6.0, also released today, includes the fix.

The flaw is in the files session handler, which PHP uses by default. Sessions kept in Redis, a database, or another store are outside this defect, though each store's handling of a cut-off update needs its own check.

Whether an attacker needs to log in depends on how the app exposes its session handling.

On the path the company tested, the attacker uses upload progress, a PHP feature that records the progress of a file upload in the user's session. Where the feature is on, a multipart form upload that carries its field makes PHP write to the session before the app's own code runs. The app does not need an upload page, only any page that opens the session later.

PHP 8.1 and older are end-of-life. The official end-of-life mitigation branches for 7.4, 8.0, and 8.1 are not affected, but older unpatched builds of those versions are, according to the company.

The company's remediation guidance sets out these steps:

Install the fixed release for your branch, as listed above. If you run PHP 8.1 or older, move to a supported branch.

Rebuild any container images and redeploy.

Check that the PHP version serving web requests is the new one.

If you cannot update yet and your app does not use upload progress, set session.upload_progress.enabled = 0 in php.ini or the server's PHP settings, then restart the PHP workers. Also limit the untrusted data your app stores in sessions.

Switching the feature off is not a full fix. It closes the upload path, but apps that put attacker-controlled data into sessions in other ways remain affected, the maintainers say.

The maintainers listed the same setting on October 21 as a temporary measure for one of the PHP2Shell flaws. Servers that have kept it off since then still need the update.

Moving sessions to a store that replaces records in one step also avoids this defect. The update is still the recommended fix for sites that keep the files handler.

Linux distribution packages may include the fix without changing the PHP version number, according to the company, so the version number alone may not indicate whether a server is fixed.

Once installed, the fixed releases empty the session file before each write and stop the write if that step fails. A cut-off write can still lose session data, but it can no longer leave old data behind for an attacker to use.

The updates also switch upload progress off by default. The Hacker News compared PHP's source code for the October 21 releases and today's. The built-in setting is on in 8.2.35, 8.3.36, 8.4.27 and 8.5.12, and off in 8.2.36, 8.3.37, 8.4.28, 8.5.13 and 8.6.0.

The release notes do not mention or explain the change. The sample php.ini files still show the old setting in a commented-out line. A php.ini file, distribution package, or hosting panel that switches the setting on will keep it on.

Where nothing else sets it, apps that rely on upload progress will need it switched back on after the update.

The same releases also fix other PHP security flaws.

The attack starts with the session write that upload progress makes before the app's code runs, Depthfirst's researchers say in their technical analysis.

A PHP session file stores each value as a name followed by serialized data. Length fields in that data tell PHP where each string ends, and nothing in the file marks a write as finished.

PHP locks the file while it writes, but the lock cannot undo a write that stops halfway. If the worker process dies partway through, the file can retain the start of the new data and the end of the old data.

By setting the lengths in the new data, an attacker can make text that sat harmlessly inside the old data read as a separate record, including a serialized object. The next request that calls session_start() loads that record and creates the object.

The researchers' public proof uses a harmless built-in object, stdClass, which appears in the session once the file is mixed.

The emergency PHP2Shell fixes on October 21 addressed an earlier attack via the same step.

"The original exploit stopped working, but the underlying problem was still there," the researchers say of the PHP2Shell patch.

That exploit needed objects to become active while PHP recorded upload progress, so the maintainers limited which classes can load at that moment. The change did not touch the file write. The object data still reaches the disk and becomes active later, when an ordinary request calls session_start().

The attack depends on stopping the write at the right moment. The maintainers say one way is a hard memory-limit failure in another thread of a threaded PHP build, which can kill the process mid-write.

Code execution then still needs a suitable gadget chain in the app, and the flaw can also corrupt session state.

In the researchers' own Linux tests, PHP's execution timeout fired only after the write had finished, so they used an out-of-memory kill to stop the worker mid-write instead.

The planted object appeared on the 11th attempt in a fresh run. That count measures getting the file into the right state on their servers, not how often code execution succeeds.

Their own chain needed no gadget from the app, the researchers say. It used a second bug instead, memory corruption in one of PHP's built-in classes.

They are holding back its details for coordinated disclosure and have not said whether that bug is fixed in today's releases.

The same trick can also replace ordinary session values, such as who is logged in, their permissions, or a CSRF token, before the app checks them, the researchers say.

(XXXXXXX, AÇIK - https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/phpaftershell-explained-how-hackers.html, thehackernews.com, 19.11.2026)

[6] The Hacker News - PHP Fixes 7 Critical Flaws, Including Bugs That Could Allow Remote Code Execution
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Thu, 19 Nov 2026 20:48:07 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/php-fixes-7-critical-flaws-including.html

[TAM METİN - 891 kelime]
The PHP Foundation has released security updates to address multiple critical security flaws that could be exploited to achieve remote code execution on affected deployments, including CVE-2026-115560.

The release of the patch follows an advance notice in which the project maintainers alerted users to multiple critical vulnerabilities affecting all supported PHP versions under specific conditions, including non-default configuration or uncommon setups.

At a high level, successful exploitation of the defects could pave the way for remote code execution, session corruption, denial-of-service, memory corruption, or local privilege escalation.

The list of vulnerabilities is as follows -

CVE-2026-115560 (CVSS score: 9.2) - An improper input validation vulnerability that could result in remote code execution, file modification, session corruption, or denial-of-service. (Fixed in PHP 8.2.36, 8.3.37, 8.4.28, 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

CVE-2026-129182 (CVSS score: 9.2) - A use-after-free vulnerability that could result in memory corruption and possible full remote code execution, as in the case of CVE-2026-109619. (Fixed in PHP 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

CVE-2026-126714 (CVSS score: 9.2) - A Time-of-check Time-of-use (TOCTOU) vulnerability that could result in the execution of arbitrary code with the privileges of the PHP worker. Successful exploitation requires an attacker's influence over the session data, an interruption at a useful point in the write, and an application with a suitable deserialization gadget chain. (Fixed in PHP 8.2.36, 8.3.37, 8.4.28, 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

CVE-2026-129289 (CVSS score: 9.2) - An inconsistent interpretation of HTTP requests vulnerability that could result in execution of attacker-controlled PHP code as the worker without another user's interaction or a non-default PHP configuration. Successful exploitation requires a front end that enables persistent backend connections and an application path that opens "php://input" without reading it fully, such as nginx with fastcgi_keep_conn on, Apache mod_proxy_fcgi with enablereuse=on, HAProxy FastCGI applications that retain backend connections, and LSAPI. (Fixed in PHP 8.2.36, 8.3.37, 8.4.28, 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

CVE-2026-129474 (CVSS score: 9.2) - A use-after-free vulnerability that could result in remote code execution on PHP 8.5 and later. Successful exploitation depends on the application and requires control over enough request data to influence memory allocation, a reachable magic method call, and a fatal error at the relevant point. (Fixed in PHP 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

CVE-2026-129481 (CVSS score: 9.2) - A use-after-free vulnerability that could cause an application's session cleanup to fail and potentially corrupt memory, crash a reused PHP worker, or potentially allow remote code execution. (Fixed in PHP 8.3.37, 8.4.28, 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

CVE-2026-129496 (CVSS score: 9.2) - A use-after-free vulnerability that could allow an unauthenticated remote attacker to crash PHP workers or execute arbitrary code with their privileges on an affected deployment. (Fixed in PHP 8.2.36, 8.3.37, 8.4.28, 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

CVE-2026-129291 (CVSS score: 8.8) - A use-after-free vulnerability that could result in local privilege escalation. Successful exploitation requires local code execution in an FPM worker, an affected pool with catch_workers_output enabled, the epoll event backend, and the ability to manipulate worker lifetimes and retain inherited pipe descriptors. (Fixed in PHP 8.2.36, 8.3.37, 8.4.28, 8.5.13, 8.6.0)

Depthfirst, which has codenamed CVE-2026-126714 "phpAfterShell," has described it as a critical flaw that can enable a remote attacker to run arbitrary code on affected servers.

"If a pwrite()/ftruncate() update is interrupted, PHP may leave behind a malformed session file that is later loaded by session_start(), yielding an arbitrary deserialization primitive," Depthfirst said. "Chained with another of our PHP-native deserialization-to-RCE findings, this leads directly to remote code execution."

Put differently, the vulnerability turns an interrupted file write into a potential remote code execution primitive by manipulating how PHP writes session data to disk, causing it to interpret previously harmless data as executable object data.

It then exploits the unexpected session state where the PHP worker either dies while the write process is underway, leaving a partially updated file, or dies after the write is complete, but before content from the previous session is removed. Both scenarios lead to a mixture of two different session states.

"Depending on where the interruption happens, part of the new Session may already have been written while bytes from the previous Session remain on disk," security researcher Yuhang Wu said. "The result can be a Session file containing the prefix of the new serialized data and the tail of the old one."

"By carefully choosing the length fields in the new prefix, an attacker can make PHP's Session decoder interpret leftover bytes that were previously just part of an ordinary string as separate Session entries and serialized objects. Nothing needs to be deserialized during the upload-progress callback itself. The attacker only needs to leave the right bytes on disk and wait for the application to call session_start(), at which point PHP reads the modified Session and deserializes those objects."

Once the prefix of one valid session is spliced with the tail of a replacement session, a subsequent session treats the corrupted data as a legitimate server-side state. This, in turn, causes PHP to interpret it as a "different, valid Session" and ultimately enable remote code execution by chaining it with a memory-corruption in a PHP-native class.

It affects any application that runs an affected PHP version with file-based sessions. If immediate patching is not possible, it's advised to turn off session upload progress.

Patches for the flaws are available in the following versions -

Users who are using existing 8.2.x, 8.3.x, 8.4.x, and 8.5.x versions of PHP are advised to update to the latest version for optimal protection.

(XXXXXXX, AÇIK - https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/php-fixes-7-critical-flaws-including.html, thehackernews.com, 19.11.2026)

[7] The Hacker News - Drupal Patches Critical Installer Flaw That Could Give Unauthenticated Attackers Control of Sites
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Thu, 19 Nov 2026 20:03:06 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/drupal-patches-critical-installer-flaw.html

[TAM METİN - 497 kelime]
Under specific conditions, a critical vulnerability in Drupal core can allow an attacker without login credentials to use the built-in installer to attempt to reinstall a site.

That can lead to a site takeover or remote code execution, and the project rates the vulnerable configurations as uncommon. Fixed releases for all supported branches shipped yesterday.

The Drupal Security Team disclosed the flaw, tracked as CVE-2026-132165, yesterday and rated it critical, 18 out of 25 on the project's risk scale.

The installer ships in every copy of Drupal core as the file /core/install.php and stays on the site after installation. Core's default .htaccess rules do not block requests to it, so blocking it is left to each site.

No attacks are reported in the security advisory, which rates exploitation as complex and records an existing proof-of-concept exploit. Some exploit paths are blocked where the web server cannot write to or change permissions on settings.php.

The team had told site owners in an advance notice earlier this week to set aside time for the update, warning that exploits may be developed within hours or days.

Every release below 10.6.22 is affected, along with Drupal 11 releases from 11.0.0 before 11.3.22 and from 11.4.0 before 11.4.13.

The fixed versions, detailed in the project's release notes, are:

Drupal 11.4.x: update to 11.4.13

Drupal 11.3.x: update to 11.3.22

Drupal 10.6.x: update to 10.6.22

The team recommends blocking the installer even after updating. Sites that cannot update yet should follow the project's installer guidance, updated yesterday, which describes three options: a deny rule in the web server configuration, an .htaccess rule, or removing the file and keeping it removed with Composer scripts.

Sites that use the Drupal Steward protection service are already protected from known attack vectors, according to the team, and should still update in case new vectors emerge.

Drupal 11.2.x and earlier, 10.5.x and earlier, and Drupal 8 and 9 are end-of-life and get no fix. The 11.3.x branch and Drupal 10 lose security coverage altogether in December under the project's support schedule.

In the week of November 8, 154,485 of the 528,430 sites reporting usage data to drupal.org, about 29 percent, were running end-of-life branches.

The flaw was found by Anthropic's OSS Scanner, an automated service the AI company launched on October 8 to look for vulnerabilities in open-source projects that opt in. Findings go to maintainers without human review, each with a reproducer and, where available, a candidate patch, according to its launch announcement.

Drupal's security team said on November 2 that a surge of security reports, which it attributes to AI tools, is more than it can sustain at its current funding, and that it is cutting security coverage for core minor versions from 12 months to 8 starting in 2027.

This is the second critical remote code execution flaw fixed in Drupal core in two months, after the Field UI flaw patched on October 14, which also had at least one exploit path that needed no login.

[8] The Hacker News - Everything Is a Web Application, Including Your Firewall
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Thu, 19 Nov 2026 17:22:23 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/everything-is-web-application-including.html

[TAM METİN - 963 kelime]
One of the early targets of PHP2Shell exploitation was the firewall. On October 28, Palo Alto Networks confirmed that attackers were exploiting the two PHP vulnerabilities, CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619, against PAN-OS management interfaces exposed to the internet. Both flaws let anyone execute code on the web server without authentication. The disclosure itself went a little wrong. Details leaked on October 20, ahead of the coordinated release, and the PHP project shipped an emergency patch the next day.

The vulnerabilities require no authentication, but they are not easy to exploit reliably. It takes skill and additional knowledge about the target system. Where it matters a lot is appliances, which run identical, often outdated software that an attacker can study in advance. That is the bigger picture. An awful lot of web applications control critical devices in your network. Almost anything is a web application to some extent. That often goes unrecognized, and as a result those applications aren't adequately protected.

Everything is a web application. That is the motto of the SANS course I teach, SEC522: Application Security: Securing Web Applications, APIs, and Microservices. Web application security has traditionally been treated as a developer issue, yet anyone protecting a network must understand how web applications work to properly defend them.

Why Appliances Are the Softer Target

On ordinary web servers, address space layout randomization (ASLR) is one of the features that makes exploitation harder. It shuffles where code and data sit in memory, so an exploit can't rely on fixed addresses. VulnCheck's early analysis found the bugs exploitable on ARM64 systems and on x86 systems without ASLR. Few public exploits bypassed ASLR on x86-64, and the one VulnCheck confirmed worked only against a single PHP build. VulnCheck's own exploit chained a memory leak to get code execution with ASLR enabled.

Web application security tends to focus on large, cloud-based applications. It's often the little things, the mosquitoes on the internet, that do you in. Appliances run predictable, often outdated software, and they are everywhere.

HTTP Is More Complex Than Your Defenses Assume

HTTP is a very complex protocol with lots of options. To upload a file, a client doesn't have to send it in one piece. It can send the body in chunks without declaring its total length, and, if the server decompresses request bodies, compress it, which makes it difficult for a web server to calculate how much memory it needs to store it. Memory-safety bugs remain common as a result, in web applications and in the web servers running them. In PHP2Shell, a crafted multipart upload pushes PHP past its memory limit while it is still parsing the request, before any application code runs. The bugs are in what PHP leaves behind when it aborts the request: uninitialized request memory (CVE-2026-109618) and a pointer to freed memory (CVE-2026-109619). The request doesn't need to be large, either. Deeply nested, empty file fields make PHP allocate far more memory than the body contains, so a request well under the default 8 MB post_max_size can still exhaust the default 128 MB memory limit.

Defenders must understand the protocol to know what these vulnerabilities look like, for example to write signatures that detect the exploits. The same complexity limits the middle boxes we rely on. They can even interfere in ways nobody planned. Intruder found that nginx in front of PAN-OS buffered chunked request bodies, which broke public exploit chains. Web application firewalls are often not adequate, and file uploads are key functionality for a lot of web applications, so blocking them outright isn't an option.

That takes out many of the traditional defenses. What remains is knowing where your critical web applications are. Identify them, starting with the admin interfaces on your firewalls and other network equipment, and restrict access to them. Palo Alto's own guidance for PHP2Shell was to remove internet access to management interfaces and limit them to trusted internal IP addresses. If you rely on a web application firewall, configure it properly and understand how much protection it does and doesn't provide.

Risk Was the Hardest Call

The clearest lesson from PHP2Shell is how difficult it can be to accurately determine the risk from a vulnerability like this. Prioritization is always a big issue for defenders, particularly with today's flood of vulnerabilities, and this one was hard to read from every angle.

The scores told different stories. The PHP project rated both flaws 9.2 under CVSS 4.0, and Fortinet's PSIRT listed 9.8 under CVSS v3. Palo Alto initially scored the issue 8.7 for its own products and listed it at moderate urgency, raised it to 9.2 Critical with the highest urgency on October 27, and confirmed exploitation a week after its advisory went out. Many public proofs of concept didn't work, so a flood of attempts in your logs said little about whether a given server was at risk. And PHP core developer Gina Peter Banyard warned that one early workaround, turning off session upload progress, was not enough on its own.

We are adding these lessons to SEC522: how to assess risk, how protections like ASLR reduce it, and why appliances remain softer targets.

Defending What Runs on HTTP

I will be teaching SEC522: Application Security: Securing Web Applications, APIs, and Microservices at SANS Cyber Defense Initiative 2026, December 14-18 in Washington, D.C., and Live Online. The course starts with how HTTP works, including HTTP/2 and HTTP/3, and covers file upload handling, API security, and AI endpoints across 17 hands-on labs and a Defend the Flag capstone. It is built for the people who defend web applications, whether or not they wrote them.

Register for SEC522 at SANS Cyber Defense Initiative 2026.

[10] The Hacker News - CISA Warns of 4 Exploited Flaws Linked to Ransomware, Cryptomining, and LLMjacking Attacks
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Thu, 19 Nov 2026 13:30:00 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/cisa-warns-of-4-exploited-flaws-linked.html

[TAM METİN - 759 kelime]
The U.S. Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA) on Wednesday added four vulnerabilities impacting Synology DiskStation Manager (DSM), BerriAI LiteLLM, Apache Axis, and Vanderbilt University Research Electronic Data Capture (REDCap) to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities (KEV) catalog, citing evidence of active exploitation.

The vulnerabilities are listed below -

CVE-2026-90436 (CVSS score: 9.8) - An unsafe reflection vulnerability in Apache Axis that could allow an unauthenticated remote attacker to execute arbitrary code.

CVE-2026-90817 (CVSS score: 9.8) - A code injection vulnerability in REDCap that could allow an unauthenticated attacker to execute arbitrary code on the server.

CVE-2026-40217 (CVSS score: 8.8) - An unprotected alternate channel vulnerability in LiteLLM that could allow a remote attacker to execute arbitrary code via bytecode rewriting at the /guardrails/test_custom_code URI.

CVE-2024-10441 (CVSS score: 9.8) - An improper encoding or escaping of output vulnerability in Synology DSM that could allow a remote attacker to execute arbitrary code via unspecified vectors.

"An attacker could exploit this via crafted HTTP requests that access an unintended controller route from a public survey context and supply a crafted file-path or stream parameter during data import processing," CISA said about CVE-2026-90817. "Exploitation requires knowledge of a valid public survey hash."

The addition of CVE-2026-90436 to the KEV catalog follows reports that the flaw is exploited in conjunction with the 4EverAble2Shell vulnerabilities affecting N-able N-central to deploy cryptocurrency miners on vulnerable servers.

Rapid7, in its own analysis of the flaw, said it detected exploitation of CVE-2026-90436 against Atlassian Confluence Data Center in a customer environment by taking advantage of the fact that the corporate workspace platform uses the Axis library as part of its XML-RPC and SOAP APIs.

"To be vulnerable, the Confluence instance must have the 'Remote API (XML-RPC & SOAP)' enabled," Atlassian said. "Please note that this setting was turned off by default for new installations starting in Confluence 3.0. This feature was fully removed in Confluence 10.0."

In the activity documented by Rapid7, threat actors are said to have weaponized the flaw to run shell commands and scripts fetched from temporary Pinggy and Cloudflare Tunnel domains, followed by enumerating the compromised environment. The incident was flagged on November 13, 2026.

"Just over three minutes after the first observed execution, the attackers were authenticating to MySQL with Confluence's own database credentials, querying page bodies, application links, and attachments with names associated with credentials, tokens, VPN configurations, and private keys," Rapid7 said.

"The attacker's scripts, likely AI-generated, then targeted files in Confluence's default location for uploaded page attachments (/var/atlassian/application-data/confluence/attachments/v4/), parsed Office documents, searched for credentials, and hunted for payment card numbers, using Luhn checks to filter out false positives."

In addition, the attackers have been found to map various internal services, use a Confluence application credential to query the internal Atlassian Crowd API for users and group memberships, and attempt to carry out a Kerberos-based credential harvesting technique called AS-REP Roasting.

Although these efforts were unsuccessful, the threat actor managed to acquire a service account credential and queried a production cloud data warehouse. About 12 hours after the first exploit request, the attacker signed in to Microsoft 365 as an integration service account using legacy authentication from "192.238.253[.]176."

CVE-2026-90436, which affects Axis 1.x versions, remains unpatched as Axis 1 has reached end-of-life (EoL) status. It's recommended to migrate to a different SOAP engine, such as Apache Axis 2/Java. Alternatively, users can use a build of Axis with the patch from a July 12, 2026, commit applied.

As for CVE-2026-90817, Safe Security said in a report published late last month that it observed an unknown threat abusing the flaw to "stage a transient web shell, followed by an in-memory loader that retrieved XMRig disguised as image files, masqueraded the miner as syslog-ng, and established persistence through cron and an added SSH key."

The exploitation of CVE-2026-40217 stems from a Google Threat Intelligence Group report this week in which threat actors have leveraged the vulnerability to elevate limited-access inference keys into full administrative credentials and establish persistence as part of LLMjacking attacks aimed at financial services, retail, transportation, and technology organizations in North America, South America, and the Asia Pacific region.

Rounding off the list is CVE-2024-10441, which Synology disclosed earlier this month as being exploited in ransomware attacks targeting Network Attached Storage (NAS) devices. The attacks have been found to result in the deployment of C3RB3R ransomware.

In light of active exploitation, federal agencies have been recommended to apply fixes or necessary mitigations for DSM, REDCap, and Axis flaws by November 21, 2026, and for the LiteLLM flaw by December 2, 2026.

[TAM METİN - 488 kelime]
SBC Medical Group has warned in a filing with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) that two cybersecurity incidents exposed sensitive customer and employee information, including personal data belonging to 1.6 million people.

SBC Medical Group is a clinic management company whose main business is in Japan, but it also has a limited presence in the U.S., is traded on Nasdaq as SBC, and holds a minority stake in the medical spa chain OrangeTwist. Its clinics receive 7 million patient visits annually.

The first compromise concerns the SBC databases, while the second involves unauthorized access to clinic administrator accounts on Tribeau, a third-party booking platform.

The company discovered the database breach on November 16 and was notified of the Tribeau compromise the same day. Both incidents affected its Japan-based businesses.

"On November 16, 2026, unauthorized access occurred to certain databases within a website system managed and operated by SBC Medical Group, Ltd., the Company’s, Japanese subsidiary, in connection with its management services in Japan," reads a FORM 8-K filing with the SEC.

"The Company confirmed that an unauthorized third party accessed and extracted customer information. The method of intrusion and full scope remain under investigation."

The incident impacting SBC databases exposed basic personal information belonging to 1,624,000 people, including:

Not all of the above data types were exposed for all 1.6 million people.

Additionally, the incident exposed 255,416 reservation requests, 217,769 inquiries submitted to SBC websites, and thousands of treatment description records, image filenames, and customer reviews.

The company said the attackers also stole approximately 20,000 employee account records, including names, IDs, and passwords. For a small subset of 459 employees, the affected data also included bank account and tax information.

The second incident involved administrator accounts on the Tribeau booking platform, a service used by 56 clinics.

The operator reported access to reservation information for 12,641 people, as well as access to treatment names, dates, and fees for 6,804 people who received treatment.

SBC noted that it has not confirmed unauthorized access to electronic medical records or information from its mySBC reservation site in the database breach.

As of November 18, when the Form 8-K was submitted, the company has not seen any evidence of misuse of the exposed data.

SBC said the investigation remains ongoing, and at this time, it could not estimate the financial impact of these cybersecurity incidents.

[12] BleepingComputer - ASUS warns of critical hard-coded key flaw in Control Center Express
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Thu, 19 Nov 2026 16:15:59 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/asus-warns-of-critical-hard-coded-key-flaw-in-control-center-express/

[TAM METİN - 435 kelime]
ASUS released Control Center Express v1.7.29 to fix three critical vulnerabilities that could let remote attackers gain administrative control of the management console and the devices it manages.

Of the three, one is a maximum-severity vulnerability (CVSS score: 10.0) tracked under CVE-2026-114202, arising from a hard-coded cryptographic key used in the product.

The flaw lets an unauthenticated remote attacker forge an authentication token and gain administrator privileges, without requiring any privileges or user interaction.

The second flaw is CVE-2026-114204, also a hard-coded key problem, which lets a remote attacker with low privileges forge a session token for a recently logged-in account.

The third is CVE-2026-114205, an authorization flaw that lets a low-privileged user create an administrator account.

ASUS Control Center Express is a central dashboard that IT administrators use to monitor and remotely manage fleets of PCs.

ASUS markets it to businesses and IT service providers and lists support for more than 200 of its business, workstation, and Corporate Stable Model motherboards.

While all three vulnerabilities are rated critical, CVE-2026-114204 has a recent-session prerequisite, and CVE-2026-114205 requires a low-privileged account, whereas CVE-2026-114202 enables remote administrative access with no prerequisites.

ASUS’s security bulletin says versions earlier than v1.7.29 are affected. However, the CVE records only list affected versions through v1.7.27, leaving the status of v1.7.28 unclear.

Impacted users are advised to install v1.7.29, then update the agents on managed devices through Select Function → Client Agent Updater, and verify their versions.

Also yesterday, ASUS released security updates for Control Center Enterprise, its centralized IT management platform, and Armoury Crate, a hardware configuration utility.

Specifically, ASUS patched CVE-2026-126574, a critical authorization flaw that lets a remote user with the low-privileged Viewer role change an administrator’s password and take over the account.

The flaw affects Control Center Datacenter 4.0.0.2 and earlier and Control Center Enterprise 3.1.0.8 and earlier.

The Armoury Crate update addressed CVE-2026-112115, a high-severity privilege escalation flaw.

The flaw affects all versions up to and including 6.7.2 and could allow a low-privileged local user to trick an elevated process into loading a malicious library.

Transport for London (TfL) has announced that around 265,000 Oyster photocard customers may have had their account details exposed in a data breach.

TfL is London's public transport authority, operating the Underground, Overground, buses, trams, and the Elizabeth line. It recorded 3.53 billion passenger journeys in 2025/26, including 1.78 billion bus journeys and 1.20 billion Tube journeys.

Oyster photocards are travel smartcards with the holder's photo that give eligible passengers free or discounted travel.

The organization discovered the security incident on November 16, 2026, after receiving customer reports about emails requesting them to create an account, which came directly from TfL's mail systems as a result of a compromise.

The Oyster photocard website has now been taken offline and the organization is sending impacted individuals data breach notifications.

Security questions and answers

Security questions and answers

TfL's current assessment is that passwords, bank and payment card details, journey information, and the names and dates of birth of children with Zip Oyster cards were not compromised.

The transportation organization also said it has found no evidence so far that the affected data has been misused.

TfL estimates that around 265,000 photocard customers may have been impacted, up from an estimated 250k previously, but the investigation is not yet complete and the figure may increase further.

TfL says it has now contained the incident and contacted affected customers. It has also notified the UK's Information Commissioner's Office (ICO).

Those who received the previous email urging them to create an account on the platform should ignore it.

The leak of security questions and answers is particularly concerning, as those may be used in account access restoration, so customers should remain vigilant for suspicious activity and report it to the organization as soon as possible.

Once the Oyster photocard portal is restored, all photocard customers will be prompted to change their password, security question, and answer.

However, if those same security questions are used at other sites, they should be rotated to new questions and answers.

It is also recommended to change passwords on other services where the same information was used.

In 2024, Scattered Spider members breached TfL's network and stole customer data and disrupted online services, including Oyster refunds.

The attack cost the agency £29 million in losses and recovery costs, while two of the hackers pleaded guilty in June 2026 and were sentenced to five years and six months in prison.

[14] BleepingComputer - PHP security updates fix 18 flaws, including critical RCE bugs
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Thu, 19 Nov 2026 11:28:23 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/php-security-updates-fix-18-flaws-including-critical-rce-bugs/

[TAM METİN - 668 kelime]
The PHP dev team released security updates for 18 vulnerabilities, including critical flaws that could allow unauthenticated attackers to remotely execute code on vulnerable servers.

The updates were released Thursday, November 19, for all supported PHP branches, with PHP 8.5.13, 8.4.28, 8.3.37, and 8.2.36 now available.

Security firm Aikido reports that the updates address 18 CVEs affecting PHP's core engine, session handling, PHP-FPM, CGI, and other components.

One of the most serious flaws, tracked as CVE-2026-129289, is a critical PHP-FPM request smuggling vulnerability with a CVSS score of 9.2.

According to Aikido, the flaw allows unauthenticated attackers to execute PHP code remotely on vulnerable servers that reuse connections to PHP-FPM under certain conditions.

The vulnerability affects certain nginx, Apache, HAProxy, and LSAPI configurations that reuse backend connections to PHP-FPM.

Standard nginx and Apache configurations that do not reuse backend connections are not affected by the attack.

Other important vulnerabilities fixed in the updates include:

CVE-2026-129474: A use-after-free vulnerability involving magic method backtraces that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-129474: A use-after-free vulnerability involving magic method backtraces that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-129496: A timezone cache use-after-free vulnerability that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-129496: A timezone cache use-after-free vulnerability that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-126714: A vulnerability involving partially overwritten session files that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-126714: A vulnerability involving partially overwritten session files that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-115560: A session serializer mismatch vulnerability that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-115560: A session serializer mismatch vulnerability that could lead to remote code execution.

CVE-2026-129291: A PHP-FPM epoll use-after-free vulnerability that could lead to local privilege escalation.

CVE-2026-129291: A PHP-FPM epoll use-after-free vulnerability that could lead to local privilege escalation.

CVE-2026-128090: A PHAR OpenSSL signature bypass vulnerability involving an algorithm downgrade.

CVE-2026-128090: A PHAR OpenSSL signature bypass vulnerability involving an algorithm downgrade.

Additional fixes address memory corruption, information disclosure, cross-site scripting, denial-of-service issues, and potentially unsafe handling of HTTPS and FTPS URLs.

The releases follow a warning issued Wednesday by the PHP security team that the upcoming updates would address vulnerabilities affecting all supported PHP versions, with the highest severity rated critical.

"The issues rated CRITICAL require either non-default configuration or uncommon setups," the PHP security team warned.

One of the vulnerabilities, CVE-2026-115560, was already disclosed in an October 23 informational advisory and relates to how PHP handles session data under certain configurations, potentially leading to remote code execution.

As a temporary mitigation, PHP recommended disabling session upload progress by setting session.upload_progress.enabled=0 and restarting PHP workers. This setting was also recommended to mitigate the recent PHP2Shell flaws.

For CVE-2026-129289, administrators who cannot immediately install the security updates can mitigate the vulnerability by disabling persistent backend connections between their web servers and PHP-FPM, ensuring connections are closed after each request.

Aikido warns that these mitigations do not address all vulnerabilities fixed in the November updates and recommends ensuring PHP-FPM and LSAPI listeners are not directly exposed to untrusted networks.

Although the official security updates are limited to supported PHP branches, third-party maintainer Ondrej Surý has also released patched packages for unsupported PHP versions dating back to PHP 5.6.

Aikido has similarly released hardened PHP container images containing backported security fixes for unsupported versions, including PHP 5.6, 7.0, 7.1, 7.2, 7.4, and 8.1.

PHP administrators are urged to upgrade to PHP 8.5.13, 8.4.28, 8.3.37, or 8.2.36, depending on their supported branch, to address the newly disclosed vulnerabilities.
